## [Classifier choice is not the bottleneck in snapshot-based fault diagnosis; measurement error dominates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17998) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper systematically separates error sources in snapshot-based fault diagnosis for distribution networks, showing that with noise-free full phasor snapshots, all thirteen classifiers achieve perfect accuracy. The remaining error stems from measurement error on high-impedance faults, and three voltage sensors can classify 90-96% of faults at the 1% total vector error class, rising to 96-99% with micro-PMUs. This work clarifies that improving classifiers is not the key to better snapshot-based diagnosis; instead, reducing measurement error and strategically placing sensors are critical. It provides practical guidance for utilities deploying limited sensors in distribution networks with inverter-based resources, where fault currents are weak. The study uses three IEEE test feeders and a public dataset with distributed generation, four feature sets, and thirteen classifiers, with measurement error defined by total vector error classes. A discriminability-margin placement method selects three sensors from simulations alone in under one second and matches the best of twelve placement methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Snapshot-based fault diagnosis classifies faults from voltage phasors measured at a few buses at a single instant, offering a cheap first-stage screen. Phasor measurement units (PMUs) provide time-synchronized measurements of voltage and current phasors, and total vector error (TVE) quantifies the difference between the true phasor and its estimate, as defined in the IEEE C37.118 standard. High-impedance faults, such as downed conductors on high-resistance surfaces, produce small fault currents that are difficult to detect.

## [Quantum-Hybrid Optimization of Drilling Trajectories in Multi-Material Structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18121) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a quantum-hybrid optimization approach that uses D-Wave's Leap Constrained Quadratic Model Hybrid Solver to plan drilling trajectories on multi-material structures, minimizing total energy consumption while incorporating industrial constraints such as material grouping and tool-wear penalties. The method is benchmarked on instances ranging from 10 to 55 holes with varying material distributions and cost matrices. This work demonstrates that quantum-hybrid optimization can handle complex, multi-physics industrial constraints and generate energy-efficient drilling sequences under realistic manufacturing conditions, potentially leading to reduced energy consumption and tool wear in industries such as aerospace and automotive manufacturing. It also adds to the growing body of evidence on the practical applicability of quantum annealing for constrained routing problems. The problem is formulated as a constrained routing problem with open-path routing and mandatory grouping of holes by material to avoid frequent tool-bit changes, and the hybrid solver handles binary, discrete, integer, and real variables. The benchmarks cover 10 to 55 holes, but the paper does not compare against classical solvers or provide runtime analysis, leaving the quantum advantage unclear.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Quantum annealing is a metaheuristic for solving combinatorial optimization problems by exploiting quantum fluctuations, and D-Wave Systems offers cloud access to quantum annealers. The Leap Constrained Quadratic Model Hybrid Solver combines quantum and classical resources to tackle constrained problems that may not fit directly on quantum hardware. Drilling trajectory optimization in multi-material structures is a practical manufacturing problem where the order of drilling holes affects energy consumption and tool wear, especially when holes in different materials require different drill bits.

## [JFinBench: A Continuously Refreshable Japanese Financial Benchmark from Certification Exams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18130) ⭐️ 6.0/10

JFinBench introduces a Japanese financial benchmark of 321 examples drawn from the 2026 editions of six certification exams covering accounting, tax, financial planning, and banking, built via a human-in-the-loop agentic pipeline. The pipeline uses a coding agent to write exam-specific conversion code and a review page, while a human verifies every example and feeds corrections back to the agent. Existing financial benchmarks offer limited coverage of country-specific financial systems and are hard to keep current as released questions leak into training data. JFinBench's refreshable design mitigates data contamination and provides a more accurate evaluation of LLMs on Japanese financial knowledge, which matters for model developers and financial institutions operating in Japan. Evaluating 12 recent LLMs, Claude Fable 5 and GPT-6 Astra exceed 93% overall, while the best open-weight model, Kimi K3.1 Pro, trails Claude Fable 5 by 12 percentage points. When models are given terminal and web access, every model improves and the gap narrows to about 4 points, but the model ranking stays largely the same, suggesting that knowledge held in the model itself remains valuable even with tools.

## [Causal AI and Defensive VAE Secure mmWave Beam Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18321) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a causally-aware deep learning framework that integrates causal discovery and a defensive variational autoencoder (DVAE) to improve the security and efficiency of mmWave beam alignment. The two-stage causal beam selection algorithm learns a Bayesian graph over RSSI inputs and the optimal beam index, reducing beam-sweeping overhead by 59.4% and 89.8% compared to conventional DL-based selection and exhaustive search, respectively, while the DVAE defense sustains 85% accuracy under adversarial perturbations. This work addresses two critical gaps in deep learning-based beam alignment for mmWave communications: poor interpretability and vulnerability to adversarial attacks on received power measurements. By combining causal discovery with a defensive autoencoder, it offers a path toward more robust and efficient beam management in 5G/6G networks, where beam alignment overhead and security are major concerns. The causal beam selection achieves accuracy comparable to conventional methods while providing 829× lower per-sample computational cost than SHAP-based feature selection, and the DVAE defense surpasses defensive distillation by 15–20% on average under different attack scenarios. However, results are based on simulations, and real-world deployment challenges such as channel dynamics and hardware impairments are not addressed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Millimeter-wave (mmWave) communication uses high-frequency bands to achieve high data rates, but it requires precise beam alignment between transmitter and receiver to overcome high path loss. Traditional beam alignment relies on exhaustive search or deep learning-based classifiers that use received signal strength indicators (RSSI) to predict the best beam, but these methods can be computationally expensive and vulnerable to small adversarial perturbations. Causal discovery aims to identify cause-effect relationships among variables, often represented as Bayesian graphs, to improve interpretability and generalization. Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that can learn to reconstruct clean data from noisy or perturbed inputs, making them suitable for defense against adversarial attacks.

This paper extends the SleepXAI framework to systematically compare five explainability (XAI) methods for single-channel EEG sleep staging, evaluating attribution fidelity via Spearman rank correlation with AASM-defined frequency bands on the Sleep-EDF and DREAMT datasets. Integrated Gradients (IG) consistently achieved the highest mean attribution fidelity across all five sleep stages on Sleep-EDF and across all AHI severity groups on DREAMT. Attribution fidelity is measured using Spearman rank correlation with AASM-defined frequency bands as clinically grounded reference signals, and the DREAMT dataset is stratified by Apnea-Hypopnea Index (AHI) severity into four groups.

## [IBMARL: Imitation Bootstrapped Multi-Agent RL for Cooperative Teams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18543) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IBMARL extends Imitation Bootstrapped Reinforcement Learning (IBRL) to cooperative multi-agent teams, using Round-Robin Behavior Cloning (R2BC) to train imitation policies and an ensemble of centralized critics to select between imitation and RL action proposals at each step. On four cooperative MARL benchmarks, it surpasses its imitation policies 2.6–4.2× faster than demonstration-based baselines on two tasks, is the only method to surpass them on the other two, and exceeds the demonstrator itself on two tasks. This work addresses the difficulty of collecting demonstrations for multi-agent systems by allowing a single human to control one agent at a time, potentially making imitation-accelerated MARL more practical for real-world cooperative tasks such as robot teams. It also shows that combining imitation and RL can outperform both pure imitation and pure RL, which could influence future hybrid approaches in the field. The method uses a gated imitation penalty—a soft Q-filtered behavior cloning loss—to regularize each RL policy toward its imitation policy only where the centralized critics value the imitation action more, so that after training each agent executes its RL policy alone without centralized critics. With real human demonstrations, IBMARL still surpasses imitation learning alone and does so more than twice as fast as the best baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Imitation Bootstrapped Reinforcement Learning (IBRL) is a single-agent method that combines imitation learning from demonstrations with reinforcement learning, using a critic to decide when to follow the imitation policy versus the RL policy. Extending this to multi-agent settings is challenging because a single human cannot easily teleoperate multiple agents simultaneously. Round-Robin Behavior Cloning (R2BC) solves this by having the demonstrator control one agent at a time while others follow their current policies, but the resulting imitation policies are limited by demonstration quality. Centralized critics are a common technique in multi-agent RL where the critic has access to global state information during training, enabling better coordination.

## [ProdPilot: MCP Server Automates Production Readiness for AI-Coded Projects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17381) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ProdPilot is a local Model Context Protocol (MCP) server that audits Node.js and React projects against 50 production-readiness rules, repairs violations through a bounded loop, gates the result, and deploys it. The system achieves 88% of fixes without model-written content, using templates and extracted values, and was evaluated on 499 real repositories. This approach addresses a critical gap in AI-assisted development: projects generated by natural-language prompts often fail on first deployment due to operational issues like exposed credentials or missing security middleware. By automating production readiness with mostly template-based repairs, ProdPilot reduces variability and improves reliability for developers using AI coding tools. Of the 50 rules, 28 fixes are fixed templates, 16 are filled with project-extracted values, and only 6 require model-written content; across 499 repositories, 93.5% of violations fall into the non-model classes. Independent verification rejected 25 of 237 fixes reported as applied, and a deployability model trained on 684 real deployments achieved 0.909 ROC AUC.

**Background**: Vibe coding refers to AI-assisted development where natural-language prompts generate working applications, often without deep technical oversight. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard that lets AI agents interact with external tools and data sources, enabling local servers like ProdPilot to provide specialized capabilities to IDE agents such as VS Code, Cursor, and Windsurf.

## [Ensembling Routers in Mixture-of-Experts Models: Benefits and Costs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17472) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper investigates whether ensembling routers in mixture-of-experts (MoE) models improves performance, finding that more routers lower validation loss and that training routers to maximize expert diversity helps on three of four tasks. The authors introduce a performance-diversity decomposition for router ensembles and show that combining router weights with the arithmetic mean outperforms the geometric mean. This work connects ensemble theory to MoE routing, offering a systematic analysis that could inspire new routing strategies in MoE research. However, experiments are limited to underparameterized networks on small tasks, so practical impact on large-scale models remains to be seen. The study uses underparameterized networks on two image-classification and two tabular-regression tasks, and finds that explicitly maximizing router diversity has no effect on performance. The benefit of ensembling is mild and offset by the computational cost of training additional routers.

## [GraphScent Reveals Directional Asymmetry in LLM Code Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17527) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces GraphScent, a training-free search controller that leverages a systematic directional relevance asymmetry found when using frozen open-weight LLMs (Gemma 4) for stateless code localization. The study, across 211 Python issues in 86 repositories, shows that relevance scores on enclosing scopes and upstream callers are informative, while downstream callees, peer methods, and historical co-change partners remain near chance. This finding could improve code localization for local, single-GPU LLM deployments by focusing on structural relationships that actually carry signal, potentially making automated bug fixing more efficient. It also highlights a limitation of current LLMs in understanding downstream code dependencies, which may guide future model design. The asymmetry is confirmed via zero-leakage masked-scope probes and regressions controlling for lexical similarity and snippet length, attributed to functional roles: callers expose symptom entrypoints delegating to defective routines, while downstream helpers appear benign alone. GraphScent propagates relation-conditioned log-likelihood ratios over Typed-AST multigraphs and uses a listwise comparison, lifting exact localization on lexically hard targets from 12.8% to 23.4% at 31B parameters within a single-GPU budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Code localization is the task of identifying the specific code entities (e.g., functions, methods) that need to be modified to fix a bug or implement a change. Gemma 4 is a family of open-weight LLMs released by Google DeepMind in April 2026, known for their on-device capabilities.

## [Binarization in Weightless Neural Networks Creates Adversarial Attack Surface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17646) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper analyzes how the binarization step in weightless neural networks (WNNs) such as WiSARD, Bloom WiSARD, and BTHOWeN creates an attack surface for adversarial perturbations. The authors formalize threshold-crossing perturbations with a threshold-crossing bound and a response-margin certificate, and show experimentally that encoding granularity can trade clean accuracy for robustness. This work challenges the assumption that WNNs are naturally robust because small perturbations often leave binary inputs unchanged, showing that threshold-crossing attacks can still flip predictions. It matters for security-sensitive deployments of lightweight classifiers, such as intrusion detection on resource-constrained devices, and provides a formal foundation for certifying their robustness. The paper introduces a threshold-crossing bound and a response-margin certificate for standard Boolean WiSARD, and evaluates linear versus data-driven thermometer encodings under model-agnostic sensitivity probes, a score-based Simulated Annealing attack, and surrogate-transfer attacks on MNIST. On Bot-IoT, CICIDS, and UNSW-NB15, adversarial perturbations cause WNN detectors to misclassify many attacks as normal traffic and raise false alarms even when aggregate accuracy remains high.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Weightless neural networks (WNNs) are lightweight classifiers that binarize inputs and store binary patterns in lookup memories (RAM-based models), making them attractive for resource-constrained tasks like intrusion detection. WiSARD is the canonical RAM-based WNN, and variants such as Bloom WiSARD and BTHOWeN replace lookup memories with Bloom filters to reduce memory. Adversarial examples are inputs deliberately perturbed to cause misclassification, and binarization is often thought to provide inherent robustness because small perturbations may not cross any threshold.

## [Active learning of LTLf specs via distinguishing queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17866) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces an active-learning framework for LTLf temporal specifications where the teacher only labels traces and answers natural-language questions, using distinguishing queries and version-space ranking. It proves consistency, identification within bounded hypothesis classes with a query bound, and a PAC guarantee, and experiments on 36 targets show identification with fewer than seven queries beyond an initial sample of ten traces on average. This work reduces the burden on teachers in specification mining by eliminating the need for equivalence queries and counterexample construction, which could make formal verification more accessible to non-experts. It also provides theoretical guarantees and shows that an enumeration-based version space outperforms previous SAT-based approaches, potentially influencing future active-learning tools for temporal logics. The learner maintains an enumerated version space of candidate formulas, generates traces that distinguish candidates, and uses teacher hints to rank or restrict the search; correct hints reduce queries by up to 16% and generated candidates by a fifth, and ranking hints never prevent identification even when incorrect. The enumeration-based approach is faster than the SAT-based approach of previous learners at every target size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: LTLf (Linear Temporal Logic on Finite Traces) is a logic for specifying properties of finite executions, widely used in verification and monitoring. Active learning of specifications involves a learner interacting with a teacher to infer a formula from examples, but traditional approaches require the teacher to answer equivalence queries and provide counterexamples, which can be difficult. Version space is a classic concept in active learning that represents all hypotheses consistent with the data, and distinguishing queries select inputs that split the version space.

## [UltraBERT-JEV: Encoder Models for System One Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17867) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv technical report compares four fine-tuning recipes for UltraBERT-380M to perform single-pass option selection, achieving 69.7–72.1% test accuracy versus 67.6% for Qwen3-0.6B. The best recipe scores options by 'yes' minus 'no' and adds sigmoid normalization, reaching 71.3% on the test set and 59.6% on external benchmarks. This work shows that small encoder models can outperform larger decoder LLMs on constrained multiple-choice tasks with lower latency, offering a practical alternative for latency-sensitive 'System One' applications. It also provides concrete benchmarks comparing encoder and decoder approaches, which can guide architecture choices for efficient inference. On an NVIDIA A100 with one request at a time, the encoder takes 29 ms per decision versus 68 ms for the decoder, but uses more peak memory (2.8 GiB vs. 2.2 GiB). An auxiliary head trained on human-marked evidence and SelfCite-adapted reward labels predicts supporting sentences in the same pass.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: UltraBERT-380M is a 380M-parameter pre-norm Transformer encoder with RoPE, SwiGLU, RMSNorm, and a native 32K context, designed for classification and retrieval. 'System One' refers to fast, intuitive decision-making, contrasting with slower deliberative 'System Two' reasoning. The LLM-as-Jev recipe is a fine-tuning objective that optimizes candidate selection via a tree-factorized listwise loss, and DeepSeek-V3's expert gate is a mixture-of-experts routing mechanism.

## [Two-Stage Photogrammetric 3D Reconstruction Improves Animal Behavior Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17881) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.17881) proposes a two-stage photogrammetric approach for animal behavior classification, where 3D body and object geometry is reconstructed before behavior is classified. The method is demonstrated on simulated interacting mice and extended to real video. This work challenges the dominant end-to-end video-based behavior estimation paradigm by making the ill-posed 2D-to-3D inverse problem explicit and potentially correctable, which could improve robustness and interpretability in computer vision and animal behavior research. The approach is currently limited to a niche application, with results shown on simulated mice and an extension to real video, indicating early-stage validation. The two-stage design separates geometry reconstruction from classification, allowing ambiguities in the 2D-to-3D mapping to be addressed explicitly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Photogrammetry is the science of obtaining reliable 3D information from photographic images, often by stitching multiple photos into models. In computer vision, recovering 3D structure from 2D images is an ill-posed inverse problem because multiple 3D configurations can produce the same 2D projection. Traditional animal behavior classification often uses end-to-end deep learning on video, which may implicitly handle this ambiguity without making it explicit.

## [MineralExp: New Benchmark for LLM Agents in Geochemical Experiment Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17931) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced MineralExp, a benchmark that reformulates geochemical experimental design as a hypothesis-based experiment selection problem, using a taxonomy of 19 experimental types across five mineral-system processes. It contains 277 cases and 1,572 reference experimental plans, and evaluation shows current LLM agents achieve high recall but low precision, proposing more experiments than necessary. This benchmark addresses a relatively unexplored upstream task in geoscience—automated experimental design—which could help reduce costly laboratory work by guiding which experiments to conduct. It also provides a structured way to evaluate LLM agents in domains with methodological diversity and subjective scoring challenges. The benchmark abstracts concrete instrument names and procedural details into a shared taxonomy, enabling deterministic multi-label targets while accommodating diverse protocols. Scoring uses multi-label classification metrics and an LLM-as-a-Judge metric, and the dataset is constructed from published ore-deposit literature and verified by domain experts.

**Background**: In mineral exploration, verifying geological hypotheses relies on designing geochemical experiments, which is an upstream step before costly laboratory work. Evaluating such capability is difficult because geochemical experiments exhibit high methodological diversity and subjectivity, and open-ended plans resist structured labeling.

## [AG-JEPA: Anatomically Guided JEPA for Alzheimer's MRI Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18097) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AG-JEPA introduces anatomical tokens into a Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) to learn region-specific brain MRI representations for Alzheimer's disease, and its extension Prog-AG-JEPA models disease progression as a conditioned latent displacement. On the MR-RATE dataset, AG-JEPA achieves an 8.4 percentage point AUROC gain over the strongest baseline on CN/MCI classification and a 7.1 percentage point gain on out-of-distribution CN/AD classification. This work shows that injecting anatomical priors into self-supervised JEPA models can improve early detection and prognosis of Alzheimer's disease, particularly in the subtle transition from cognitively normal to mild cognitive impairment. It could benefit medical imaging researchers and clinicians working on neurodegenerative disease diagnosis and cross-cohort generalization. AG-JEPA adds anatomical tokens that interact with patch tokens during pretraining, and Prog-AG-JEPA models progression as a conditioned latent displacement in the learned representation space. The method is evaluated on five downstream tasks spanning diagnosis and progression prediction, but the results are limited to the MR-RATE dataset and require further validation on larger, diverse cohorts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Alzheimer's disease is a neurodegenerative disorder and the most common cause of dementia, with brain atrophy detectable on MRI before cognitive symptoms become apparent. JEPA (Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture) is a self-supervised learning framework that learns representations by predicting embeddings of masked regions rather than raw pixels. Self-supervised learning is increasingly used in medical imaging to reduce reliance on costly expert annotations.

## [PUMA-Former: Uncertainty-Aware Agentic Offloading for MEO-LEO Satellite Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18139) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose PUMA-Former, an uncertainty-aware agentic framework for cooperative computation offloading in MEO-LEO satellite networks under incomplete information. The framework uses latent-delay attention and queue-error-aware fusion to balance completed work, service delay, and execution cost, achieving an 8.62% increase in admitted compute-work completion and a 34.78% reduction in mean modeled service delay compared to ATLA-adapt. This work addresses the challenge of stale or missing CPU state reports in satellite edge computing, which can lead to inefficient offloading decisions. By explicitly handling uncertainty, the framework could improve resource utilization and service quality in dynamic satellite networks, impacting future satellite communication and edge computing systems. The framework employs a structured Transformer allocator conditioned on inferred resource moments to make joint decisions on destination, partition, bandwidth, CPU, and power. Bounded risk-aware calibration adjusts workload splits, and while service gains over learning baselines involve higher normalized energy, the approach also improves completion and normalized energy over fixed-half round-robin offloading.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Satellite edge computing distributes mobile workloads across moving processors in satellite networks, where medium Earth orbit (MEO) and low Earth orbit (LEO) satellites cooperate to provide coverage. MEO satellites are at higher altitudes (about 5,000-12,000 km) and can act as coordinators, while LEO satellites are closer to Earth and offer lower latency but require more handovers. In such networks, periodic reports from LEO satellites may be delayed or missing, making offloading decisions sensitive to uncertain capacity and congestion.

## [T2ST: Data-Efficient Spatio-Temporal Foundation Models via Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18199) ⭐️ 6.0/10

T2ST is a data-efficient training framework that augments limited spatio-temporal observations with selected auxiliary time series and transfers knowledge from a frozen time-series foundation model. Using only 10% of the original spatio-temporal data, it enables spatio-temporal foundation models to achieve forecasting performance competitive with or superior to full-data counterparts across six long-term and twelve short-term forecasting datasets. This work addresses the data scarcity problem in spatio-temporal foundation models by leveraging abundant time-series data, potentially lowering the cost of training such models. It establishes a new paradigm for data-efficient training that could benefit researchers and practitioners working with limited spatio-temporal observations. T2ST retrieves matched sequences to reinforce sparsely observed temporal patterns and selects reliable complementary sequences to broaden temporal coverage. It employs temporal relation distillation to align relationships among historical patches and multi-scale prediction distillation to transfer forecasting behavior at fine and coarse temporal resolutions, enabling knowledge transfer across heterogeneous architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Spatio-temporal foundation models (STFMs) are large pre-trained models designed for forecasting tasks involving data with both spatial and temporal dimensions, such as traffic or climate data. They typically require massive, synchronized spatio-temporal datasets with spatial metadata, which are expensive to collect. Time-series foundation models, on the other hand, are pre-trained on abundant univariate or multivariate time series and can forecast across domains. T2ST bridges these two by transferring knowledge from a time-series foundation model to an STFM when spatio-temporal data is scarce.

## [RACE: Uncertainty-Aware Adaptive Compression for Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18244) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RACE introduces a ranking-uncertainty aware, capacity-adaptive compression framework for efficient sequential recommendation that dynamically adjusts model structure and input sequences per instance. It derives a list-wise ranking-uncertainty metric from top-K prediction stability and uses a self-supervised predictor to guide instance-specific slimming via channel sensitivity extraction and progressive sequence filtering. Existing compression methods apply uniform ratios, causing a capacity-complexity mismatch that wastes resources on simple instances and under-serves complex ones. RACE's instance-adaptive approach could improve both accuracy and efficiency in real-time recommender systems deployed under resource constraints. The framework uses a hypernetwork-style channel sensitivity extractor to identify parameter redundancy and importance-aware progressive sequence filtering to mitigate data redundancy. Experiments on four popular datasets show RACE outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on accuracy, efficiency, and adaptability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Sequential recommendation models predict the next item a user will interact with based on their history, and they must often run in real-time under tight compute budgets. Model compression reduces model size and computation, but conventional methods compress all inputs uniformly. RACE argues that user intent complexity varies, so compression should adapt per instance based on how uncertain the model is about its ranking predictions.

## [ParetoM³: Preference-Guided Pareto Learning via Min-Max-Min Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18252) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ParetoM³, a single-loop first-order algorithm that solves a penalty-based min-max-min (M³) formulation to find preference-optimal solutions on the weak Pareto set of multiple nonconvex objectives. Under a local Kurdyka-Łojasiewicz assumption, it provably finds an ε-stationary point that is also approximately weakly Pareto optimal, with explicit non-asymptotic convergence rates. This work provides a theoretically grounded method for preference-guided multi-objective optimization, which is relevant to machine learning tasks where trade-offs between conflicting objectives (e.g., accuracy vs. fairness) must be navigated. The convergence guarantees and unified formulation could make Pareto-optimal learning more practical for real-world deployment. The M³ problem unifies preference optimization and weak Pareto optimality enforcement in a single objective via a merit function that vanishes on the weak Pareto set. The stationarity notion recovers existing criteria such as approximate preference stationarity and approximate KKT conditions under assumptions used in prior work, and experiments cover synthetic benchmarks, image classification, regression, and math reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-objective optimization deals with problems having several conflicting objectives, where no single solution is best for all; instead, the goal is to find the Pareto set of trade-off solutions. Preference-guided optimization seeks a solution on the Pareto set that minimizes a user-defined preference function. The Kurdyka-Łojasiewicz condition is a geometric property of objective functions that enables convergence analysis of first-order methods beyond convexity.

A new arXiv preprint (2611.18375) proposes a framework that generates shape-controlled multi-view echocardiographic sequences by extracting cross-sections from dynamic whole-heart meshes and converting them into realistic ultrasound frames using a two-stage GAN-diffusion pipeline. Stage 1 uses an unpaired GAN-diffusion model for frame-level translation, while Stage 2 applies masked video reconstruction to improve temporal coherence and cardiac motion. This work addresses the scarcity of large, well-annotated echocardiography datasets by offering a scalable way to generate synthetic multi-view data that preserves consistency across cardiac chambers, which could aid dataset augmentation and the development of multi-view cardiac assessment methods. It moves beyond left-ventricle-only or single-view approaches, potentially enabling training of more robust clinical AI models. The pipeline first extracts multi-view cross-sections from dynamic whole-heart meshes, then introduces ultrasound-like appearance via the two-stage AI pipeline; evaluation showed high frame-level realism for both stages, with Stage 2 substantially improving video-level motion quality and temporal consistency. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review and lacks community discussion.

## [Scoping Review Maps Path from Remote Monitoring to Agentic Care Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18484) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A structured scoping review (arXiv:2611.18484) finds that agentic AI care systems remain human-led and largely confined to prototypes and pilots, with stronger decision-action coupling and embodied AI not yet mature. The authors propose 'agentic robotics' as a future direction: supervised systems embedded in the care ecosystem that connect AI agents and robots with clinicians, caregivers, and patients through auditable, interruptible workflows. This review highlights that moving from passive remote patient monitoring to embodied robotic action requires safe, interoperable, and accountable care architectures, which could shape design priorities for healthcare AI and robotics researchers, clinicians, and policymakers. It underscores that human oversight and explicit AI governance are fundamental design requirements, not afterthoughts, for any transition to agentic care. The review is a synthesis rather than a novel breakthrough, and no community discussion is provided to validate its impact. It specifically argues that healthcare robotics makes the monitoring-to-action transition visible by translating AI recommendations into speech, movement, prompting, physical assistance, escalation, and care coordination, making embodiment, clinical integration, human oversight, and governance fundamental design requirements.

## [MeReader: Offline, Privacy-Preserving AI Reading Assistant with Spoiler-Free Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18513) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MeReader is a new offline, privacy-preserving AI assistant for narrative eBook reading that confines retrieval and generation to the reader's current position, preventing spoilers. It uses a hybrid retrieval architecture combining vector similarity, BM25, and summary embeddings, and achieves comprehension parity with larger models using mid-sized local LLMs (2B-4B parameters). This work shows that a localized, privacy-centric approach can augment digital reading without relying on cloud services, offering a viable alternative for users concerned about privacy and narrative continuity. It also demonstrates that mid-sized local models can deliver acceptable quality, potentially broadening access to AI-assisted reading on consumer hardware. The system identifies a quality-latency frontier, showing that models with 2B-4B parameters can match the comprehension of 5B+ models while maintaining acceptable response quality. The progress-aware boundary mechanism prevents future content leakage without compromising retrieval quality and correctness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that combines retrieval-based methods with generative AI to produce context-aware answers. MeReader applies RAG to eBook reading, but unlike conventional systems that process entire books at once, it restricts retrieval to the reader's current position to avoid spoilers. Hybrid retrieval combines multiple search methods (vector similarity, BM25, summary embeddings) to improve accuracy.

## [Sender-Receiver Framework Evaluates Chain-of-Thought Transfer Between LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17544) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.17544v2) introduces a Sender-Receiver setup to evaluate chain-of-thought reasoning transferred between LLMs, measuring reliability via Verifiability, usefulness via Correctability, and safety via Deceptiveness. The study finds that task nature most affects reliability, receiver nature most affects usefulness, and sender nature most affects safety. As AI systems increasingly compose multiple models, understanding how reasoning transfers between them is crucial for building reliable and safe multi-agent pipelines. This work provides a systematic evaluation framework that can inform the design of inter-model communication protocols and safety safeguards. The Sender and Receiver LLMs do not know each other's identity, preventing the Sender from tailoring its reasoning to a specific Receiver. Results show reliability is higher on arithmetic tasks and lower on science and commonsense tasks, and all three factors (task, sender, receiver) affect all three metrics.

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting is a technique where LLMs generate intermediate reasoning steps before producing a final answer, improving performance on complex tasks. In multi-agent systems, one model's output (including its reasoning) is often passed to another model, but the reliability, usefulness, and safety of such transferred reasoning are not well understood. This paper systematically evaluates these aspects using a controlled Sender-Receiver setup.

## [Regression-Free Layout-Aware Matching Eliminates GUI Grounding Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09654) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.09654v2) proposes a regression-free framework where a frozen multimodal LLM parses user instructions into layout-rich visual descriptions, and a dedicated Layout-Aware GUI Grounding Model performs precise localization by matching against layout-prior candidates without learning any coordinate regression. The method achieves over 20% improvement in grounding accuracy on ScreenSpot-Pro and raises success rate and element selection rate by more than 15% on Mind2Web compared to end-to-end systems. GUI agents are increasingly relying on purely visual MLLMs that operate directly on screenshots, but coordinate hallucinations from deficient fine-grained perception remain a persistent obstacle. By decoupling instruction understanding from localization, this approach offers a way to suppress hallucinations without expensive fine-tuning, potentially making GUI automation more reliable and accessible. The grounding model is trained with only Text/Icon binary labels and requires no coordinate regression parameters, avoiding costly fine-tuning of the MLLM. The approach relies on a frozen MLLM for instruction parsing and a separate layout-aware matching module, which inherently suppresses hallucinations by selecting from layout-prior candidates rather than regressing coordinates directly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: GUI grounding is the task of translating abstract user instructions (e.g., "click the settings icon") into precise element coordinates on a screen, a core capability for GUI agents that automate software interaction. Conventional grounding models often lack semantic richness to interpret abstract instructions, while end-to-end multimodal LLMs suffer from coordinate hallucinations due to insufficient fine-grained visual perception. Regression-based approaches learn to predict coordinates directly, which can amplify these hallucinations when the model's perception is imperfect.

## [LLM-Generated Compliance Artifacts Tested for DPPs and DPIAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21317) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.21317v2) critically evaluates how LLMs generate regulatory compliance artifacts—Digital Product Passports (DPPs) and Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIAs)—in industrial settings, benchmarking multiple models against manually created ground-truth schemas. The study finds that looser guidelines like DPIA formatting need richer context prompts to stay consistent and complete, while stricter formats like Digital Battery Passports yield consistent output regardless of prompt context but risk more hallucinations. As EU regulations like ESPR and GDPR push companies to produce standardized compliance documentation, this work shows that LLM assistance is viable but not uniformly reliable, which matters for manufacturers, compliance teams, and tool builders deciding where automation can safely be applied. It also highlights a practical trade-off between regulatory strictness and hallucination risk that will shape how compliance-generation systems are designed and audited. The evaluation benchmarks different LLMs against manually created ground-truth schemas, focusing on how data extraction instructions and regulatory vagueness affect output quality and consistency. ## [MeanFlowAdvantage Aligns Reward Fine-Tuning with Few-Step Average-Velocity Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37670) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MeanFlowAdvantage introduces a signed advantage-weighted least-squares objective for reward fine-tuning of few-step MeanFlow generators, using a shared detached MeanFlow derivative correction to express the reward in prediction space. On SD3.5-Medium, it improves all eight reported metrics over a matched four-step MeanFlowNFT baseline and matches or exceeds a 40-step DiffusionNFT baseline on six of eight metrics with only four NFEs. This addresses a real mismatch between the average-velocity representation used at inference and the instantaneous-velocity or x0-space objectives used in existing advantage-based reward fine-tuning. It could make reward fine-tuning more effective for fast few-step diffusion models, benefiting practitioners who need high-quality generation with very few sampling steps. The formulation preserves MeanFlow's native few-step sampler and makes rollout and reference regularization exact penalties on the deployed average-velocity network. The same objective also transfers to DNA promoter design, supporting teacher-free on-policy RL and teacher-guided reward-graded distillation, with the latter achieving the lowest one-step Sei profile MSE among compared configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: MeanFlow is a diffusion-model approach that enables efficient few-step generation by learning to predict interval-average velocities rather than instantaneous velocities. Reward fine-tuning adapts generative models to maximize a reward signal, often using advantage-weighted objectives inspired by reinforcement learning, but these objectives are typically defined on instantaneous velocities or equivalent x0 predictions. This creates a mismatch because inference with MeanFlow directly uses the learned average-velocity map. SD3.5-Medium is a variant of Stability AI's Stable Diffusion 3.5 family designed for consumer-grade GPUs.

A new arXiv paper presents the CKG Clinical Explanation Engine, which converts patient inference states and disease knowledge from a frozen, training-free knowledge-graph reasoner into typed facts, explicit rule traces, provenance-linked conclusions, and policy-licensed recommendations. Across six workbooks covering 11,289 patients and 33,867 patient-disease traces, the system achieved 100% validity, knowledge provenance, cross-sheet numerical fidelity, and exported fact/report field presence. The high validation metrics across a large patient dataset suggest the approach is robust, though clinical correctness and utility remain unestablished. The system distinguishes observed zero-activation and partial evidence from unobserved evidence through separate availability, representation completeness, and disease-specific activation checks. Availability-representation consistency reached 99.29% with 12,117 disagreements across five systematic feature-cohort patterns, and a seeded 30-patient end-to-end audit ran failure-free, passing all prespecified checks.

## [MOTIVE: Multi-View Self-Verification for Reliable Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07018) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.07018v2) systematically analyzes how verifier strength and prompt design affect self-verification in vision-language models, finding that stronger verifiers yield more reliable judgments while performance is highly sensitive to prompt choice with no single prompt dominating across tasks. Guided by these findings, the authors propose MOTIVE, a multi-view self-verification framework that learns a correctness-aligned reliability score to govern an accept-or-rethink decision, triggering history-guided rethinking only for uncertain answers. VLMs often produce plausible but incorrect answers, and self-verification offers a way to improve reliability without an external judge; MOTIVE's reliability-guided selective rethinking could reduce unnecessary reasoning turns while improving answer accuracy. This matters for practitioners deploying multimodal models in high-stakes settings where trustworthiness and inference efficiency are both critical. MOTIVE evaluates each candidate answer from complementary verification perspectives and trains a correctness-grounded multi-view verification model to produce a reliability score; at inference, reliable answers are returned directly while uncertain ones trigger history-guided rethinking. Experiments across diverse multimodal benchmarks and VLM backbones show consistent gains over strong self-verification and self-correction baselines, though the paper is an incremental contribution rather than a paradigm shift.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as GPT-4V, Gemini, Claude 3 Opus, LLaVA, and InstructBLIP jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models beyond text-only inputs. Self-verification is a technique where a model checks its own answers to catch errors without relying on an external judge, but existing methods typically use a single verification criterion or fixed prompt, leading to incomplete and unstable reliability estimates. Prompt engineering—designing the instructions given to a model—strongly influences verification outcomes, motivating MOTIVE's multi-perspective approach.

## [TimesJev: A System 1 Time Series Reasoning Model for Fast, Low-Latency Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26002) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.26002v3) proposes TimesJev, a System 1 time series reasoning model that uses compressed frequency-preserving representations to make fast decisions without long chains of thought. Experiments on 13 datasets show it outperforms reasoning models by over 62% while using 62.9% less inference time than their fine-tuned versions on average. This work addresses the high inference latency of LLM-based time series reasoning, which typically consumes thousands of tokens, by offering a fast alternative that could enable real-time applications. It also introduces the concept of System 1 sufficiency for time series, potentially inspiring further research into efficient time series reasoning. The method formalizes System 1 sufficiency and derives a dimension reduction objective combining a sufficiency divergence (core information lost during compression) and a realizability divergence (incompatibility of the System 1 model with the optimal prediction). It optimizes this via a gating mechanism for compression and aligns the representation with the model's embedding space.

**Background**: System 1 and System 2 thinking refer to fast, intuitive decision-making versus slow, deliberate reasoning, a concept from psychology often applied to AI. Time series reasoning with LLMs often involves processing thousands of numeric points, which is token-intensive and slow.

## [Subgroup Offsets Adjust GRPO Baseline for Heterogeneous Rollouts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00782) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes adjusting the baseline in Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) by using subgroup offsets, which are deviations of subgroup means from the global mean, controlled by a signed coefficient λ. Positive λ (Alignment) shifts credit toward a rollout's position within its subgroup, while negative λ (Contrast) shifts credit toward the subgroup's relative quality, with experiments on Qwen3-8B-Base and Olmo3-7B-Base showing modest but consistent gains in multi-sample coverage, especially for Contrast. This work addresses a key limitation of GRPO: its global baseline collapses heterogeneous rollouts onto a single credit axis, ignoring subgroup structure. The proposed method could improve policy optimization in RLHF by providing more nuanced credit assignment without changing the reward signal or objective, potentially benefiting LLM reasoning training. The adjustment acts only on the baseline, leaving the reward signal and optimization objective unchanged, and subgroups are induced by shared strategies. The method is evaluated on four competition-level math benchmarks against a λ=0 anchor that shares the same strategy-conditioned sampling, isolating the baseline effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm used in RLHF that estimates advantages by comparing rewards within a group of rollouts from the same prompt, using the group mean as a baseline. This baseline reduces variance but assumes homogeneity; in practice, rollouts may follow different strategies and form subgroups with distinct reward distributions. The paper introduces subgroup offsets to adjust the baseline accordingly.

## [Neural Field Readers Rely on Weight Coordinates, Not Visual Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05798) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05798v2) shows that neural field readers, such as HiddenProbe, can be sensitive to function-preserving weight transformations. On 10,000 CIFAR10 test images, one-period hidden-bias shifts changed 7.3% of HiddenProbe's predictions with little accuracy change, while three-period shifts changed 22.0% and reduced accuracy from 57.8% to 54.4%. This work reveals that high accuracy on original weights does not guarantee that a reader has learned the underlying visual function, which is critical for interpreting and trusting neural field representations. It affects researchers working on implicit neural representations, robustness, and representation learning, and calls for function-preserving audits when evaluating such models. The study measures recognition, prediction disagreement, and numerical fidelity together, finding that complete canonical hidden features reach 38.7% on MNIST while explicitly composing activations, outgoing weights, and output bias reaches 81.5%, indicating the information is present but the learned readout fails to recover it. A separate 720-response study of language models reading numerical weights shows that stochastic audits require repeats on both inputs because observed accuracy losses need not yield rejection of response-law equality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Neural fields are coordinate-based implicit neural representations that encode images or 3D scenes as continuous functions parameterized by neural networks. Because the same visual function can be represented by many different weight configurations (e.g., via hidden-bias shifts), a reader trained to recognize images from these weights might exploit parameter coordinates rather than the actual visual content. This paper investigates that distinction using function-preserving transformations and evaluates readers like HiddenProbe and ProbeGen.

## [AttentionSwitch: Jointly Optimizing Attention Head Selection and Scaling for LLM Jailbreak Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08752) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AttentionSwitch is introduced as the first jailbreak defense that jointly optimizes intervention sites and strengths within a frozen LLM. It learns a sparse set of attention heads along with query-conditioned multiplicative scaling factors, allowing outputs of selected heads to be attenuated or amplified without altering their representation directions. This approach addresses the suboptimality of decoupling 'where to intervene' and 'how to modify' in existing defenses, potentially leading to more effective and efficient jailbreak mitigation. It is particularly relevant for deploying LLMs safely without costly fine-tuning or catastrophic forgetting, and shows strong results when training data is scarce. AttentionSwitch operates on a frozen LLM, intervening only on a sparse set of attention heads via multiplicative scaling, which preserves the direction of representations. It outperforms existing defenses across popular models and attacks, with particularly strong performance in low-data regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks that bypass safety mechanisms to elicit harmful outputs. While fine-tuning can improve safety, it is computationally expensive and may cause catastrophic forgetting of previously learned capabilities. Recent defenses instead intervene on specific model components associated with unsafe behavior, but typically decouple the selection of intervention sites from the modification applied there, which can be suboptimal.

A new arXiv paper re-implements five readout architectures for typed-decision models and trains them under a unified protocol, finding that a 0.5M-parameter cosine head matches a 27M-parameter joint schema head in accuracy while being twice as fast at inference. The study also shows that reading all questions from one prompt is as accurate as one prompt per question, with 1.6x faster inference and 2.1-2.5x faster training on an H100. This work provides practical guidance for developers of typed-decision models, suggesting that effort should be spent on scaling the backbone rather than designing complex readout heads. It could simplify model design and reduce inference costs for applications that require structured, calibrated decisions from LLMs. The study uses Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-4B backbones, 15 public English training sources, and a per-head learning-rate sweep with the same seed. Moving from 0.6B to 4B adds 4 points in-domain and 11 points on held-out sources, while base, instruct, and thinking checkpoints tie, indicating that backbone size matters more than readout complexity or prompt strategy.

## [S1-Evo: Self-evolving attack on typed System-1 decision models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16937) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose S1-Evo, a self-evolving adversarial framework that perturbs untrusted fields to induce evidence competition in typed System-1 decision models, achieving 71.99% mean attack success rate on the jev-1.14 model across four benchmarks. This work exposes a security gap in the increasingly popular typed System-1 models used in LLM agents for routing, moderation, and authorization, showing that their contract-based decision interfaces are vulnerable to manipulation that can alter execution branches and cause end-to-end agent violations. S1-Evo uses offline feedback search guided by directional margins and re-verification to distill verified flips into experience cards, which are retrieved at test time to construct perturbations for unseen tasks; it achieves 57.47% high-confidence ASR on jev-1.14 and up to 24.00% end-to-end agent violations.

Examples include Jev by TypeSafe AI and the open-source Laya AI. Unlike text-generating LLMs, S1 models score dynamic rules and options without token-by-token decoding, so traditional adversarial attacks that target text continuation or explicit override commands do not transfer directly.

## [ChiGAD: Chi-Square Wavelet Graph Neural Network for Heterogeneous Graph Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18934) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes ChiGAD, a spectral graph neural network framework that uses a novel Chi-Square filter to improve anomaly detection in heterogeneous graphs. ChiGAD consists of a Multi-Graph Chi-Square Filter, Interactive Meta-Graph Convolution, and Contribution-Informed Cross-Entropy Loss, and its homogeneous variant ChiGNN also excels on seven GAD datasets. Existing GNN methods for graph anomaly detection mainly focus on homogeneous graphs and fail to address key challenges in heterogeneous graphs, such as capturing abnormal signals across meta-paths and handling class imbalance. ChiGAD's approach could improve anomaly detection in complex real-world systems like social networks and financial transaction networks. The framework applies dedicated Chi-Square filters to each meta-path graph to capture anomalous information, aligns features while preserving high-frequency content, and uses a contribution-informed loss to prioritize difficult anomalies. Experiments on public and industrial datasets show it outperforms state-of-the-art models, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Graph anomaly detection (GAD) aims to find unusual nodes or edges in graph-structured data, which is important for applications like fraud detection and network security. Heterogeneous graphs contain multiple types of nodes and edges, and meta-paths are sequences of node types that describe semantic relationships. Spectral graph neural networks use filters based on graph frequencies to process signals on graphs, and wavelets are mathematical tools for multi-scale analysis that inspire the Chi-Square filter design.

## [Graph Consistency Regularization: Self-Prompting with Prediction Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23373) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Graph Consistency Regularization (GCR), a framework that inserts parameter-free Graph Consistency Layers (GCLs) at arbitrary depths to align batch-level feature similarity graphs with a global class-aware masked prediction graph. The method learns adaptive weights for each layer based on graph discrepancy magnitudes, prioritizing semantically reliable layers without modifying the model architecture or training procedure. GCR offers a lightweight, model-agnostic way to improve representation learning by enforcing that feature relationships reflect class-consistent predictions, potentially leading to cleaner feature structures and better generalization across networks and datasets. This self-prompting approach could be easily integrated into existing deep learning pipelines. The GCLs are parameter-free and can be inserted after any micro-network block or specific layer, such as Inception or fully connected layers. The alignment uses a global class-aware masked prediction graph derived by modulating softmax prediction similarities with intra-class indicators, and layer importance is learned from graph discrepancy magnitudes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Deep networks learn rich representations but often capture noisy inter-class similarities that contradict predicted semantics. Graph-based regularization uses relational structures to enforce consistency, and self-prompting refers to models refining their internal structure using their own outputs. GCR combines these ideas by aligning feature similarity graphs with prediction graphs to act as a semantic regularizer.

## [Model-Free Method Quantifies Simulator Fidelity via Quantile Curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05024) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a model-free method that constructs confidence sets for latent population parameters to derive a robust proxy for the sim-to-real discrepancy, then estimates its quantile function to obtain a distribution-level risk profile of the simulator. It demonstrates the approach by evaluating the alignment of four major LLMs with human populations on the WorldValueBench dataset. As generative AI models are increasingly used to simulate real-world systems, quantifying the sim-to-real gap is critical for ensuring reliable simulations. This method provides a principled statistical framework that supports risk measures like CVaR and comparisons across simulators, which could impact how researchers validate AI simulations in social science and engineering. The method is model-agnostic and handles general output spaces, such as categorical survey responses and continuous multi-dimensional data, while accounting for heterogeneous sample sizes across scenarios. It addresses the challenge that standard predictive inference methods are ill-suited because they quantify uncertainty in observable outputs rather than latent population parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: In generative AI, sim-to-real gap refers to the discrepancy between simulated outputs and real-world data, which is crucial when AI models are used to mimic human populations or physical systems. Latent population parameters are unobserved characteristics of the real and simulated systems that cannot be directly measured, and confidence sets are statistical tools that provide a range of plausible values for these parameters. Quantile curves describe the distribution of a variable and are used here to create a risk profile of the simulator's fidelity.

## [ISLA: Ensemble Learning and Domain Adaptation for Stroke Lesion Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08732) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ISLA is an nnU-Net-based framework that combines attention modules, deep supervision, and ensemble learning across input resolutions, convolutional block types, and loss functions for acute ischemic stroke lesion segmentation in diffusion MRI. A semi-supervised variant, ISLA-MT, leverages unlabeled target-domain images and significantly improved performance on an external multicenter cohort of 590 cases. Poor generalization to unseen clinical sites is a major barrier to deploying deep learning in stroke care, and few AIS segmentation methods have been externally validated. ISLA's demonstrated robustness on an independent multicenter cohort suggests that ensemble and domain adaptation techniques can help close the gap between research models and real-world clinical use. ISLA was trained on the SOOP and ISLES 2022 datasets and evaluated on 253 internal cases and 590 external cases; it outperformed three state-of-the-art pre-trained models externally, while nnU-Net was slightly better internally but generalized worse. Both models run in about 5 seconds per case, and code and weights are publicly available on GitHub.

**Background**: Acute ischemic stroke lesions are areas of brain tissue damaged by reduced blood flow, and accurately delineating them on diffusion MRI helps guide treatment decisions. nnU-Net is a self-configuring deep learning pipeline that automatically adapts its architecture and training scheme to a given dataset, making it a strong baseline for medical image segmentation. Domain adaptation refers to techniques that help a model trained on one data distribution (source domain) perform well on a related but different distribution (target domain), such as images from another hospital. ISLES 2022 is a public benchmark dataset of ischemic stroke MRI scans with lesion masks used for training and comparison.

## [2D Projection-Derived Masks Rival 3D Segmentation for Cervical Spine Fracture Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15235) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study shows that vertebra masks approximated from 2D projections can replace full 3D segmentation in a multi-stage pipeline for cervical spine fracture recognition, achieving vertebra-level and patient-level AUPRC of 75.64% and 92.00% on the RSNA 2022 cohort. The pipeline uses YOLOv8 for detection, DenseNet121-Unet for 2D segmentation, and an ensemble of 2.5D CNN-Transformer models for classification, with no statistically significant AUPRC difference compared to using 3D segmentation masks. This work suggests that computationally expensive 3D segmentation may be unnecessary for cervical fracture recognition, potentially simplifying pipelines and reducing costs in clinical settings. It could make automated CT interpretation more accessible for rapid diagnosis of subtle injuries. The YOLOv8 detector achieved a 3D mean IoU of 94.45% for localizing the cervical spine, and the DenseNet121-Unet achieved a mean Dice of 87.86% for multi-label vertebra segmentation. The pipeline's performance differed from the reproduced RSNA 2022 winning solution by only -0.55 and +0.15 AUPRC points at vertebra and patient levels, with fold-wise 95% confidence intervals excluding losses larger than 1.46 and 2.81 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Cervical spine fractures are critical injuries that require prompt and accurate diagnosis, typically using CT scans that produce large 3D volumes. Automatic analysis is challenging because injuries can be subtle and spread across many slices. Traditional approaches often involve 3D segmentation to isolate vertebrae, but this is computationally intensive. This study explores whether 2D projections, which are simpler to process, can provide sufficient information for fracture detection.

## [EDA: Parameter- and Data-Efficient Adaptation for Speculative Decoding Draft Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09527) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Efficient Draft Adaptation (EDA), a framework that adapts draft models to fine-tuned target models using a decoupled shared/private architecture, data regeneration, and sample selection. Experiments show EDA restores speculative decoding performance on fine-tuned models with significantly reduced training costs compared to full retraining. Speculative decoding is a key technique for accelerating LLM inference, but its benefits degrade when target models are fine-tuned for specific domains. EDA provides a practical way to maintain acceleration without costly retraining, which could make speculative decoding more viable for domain-specific deployments. EDA's decoupled architecture updates only a lightweight private component while sharing the rest, and its data regeneration strategy uses the fine-tuned target model to regenerate training data to better align training with speculative decoding. A sample selection mechanism further prioritizes high-value data, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding speeds up LLM inference by using a small draft model to propose multiple tokens that a larger target model then verifies in parallel, with the average acceptance length determining the speedup. When the target model is fine-tuned for a specific domain, its output distribution shifts, causing the draft model's proposals to be accepted less often and reducing the acceleration. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods like adapters and LoRA avoid full retraining by updating only a small number of parameters, and EDA applies similar ideas to adapt draft models efficiently.

## [3D vs 2.5D U-Net Backbones in EDM for MRI Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14667) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper compares a 3D convolutional U-Net and a 2.5D slice-conditioned U-Net as backbones within an elucidated diffusion model (EDM) for 2x in-plane brain MRI super-resolution. Trained on 500 subjects from the NKI cohort of FOMO60K, the two architectures achieve nearly identical performance (37.72 dB vs 37.70 dB PSNR) on the training degradation, but show different strengths under distribution shifts. The finding that neither architecture is clearly superior suggests that for MRI super-resolution with diffusion models, the choice between 3D and 2.5D backbones may depend on the expected test conditions rather than a universal winner. This has practical implications for medical imaging researchers designing SR pipelines, as 2.5D models are often more memory-efficient and easier to train. On the training degradation, both models are 0.2 dB ahead of EDSR; with k-space truncation, EDSR matches the diffusion models; with added noise, the 2.5D model is most accurate; and on an external IXI dataset, the 3D model leads by 0.4 dB over the best baseline and 0.9 dB over the 2.5D model in foreground PSNR. All models were trained from scratch to convergence with subject-level statistics for evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: MRI super-resolution aims to computationally enhance low-resolution scans, offering a software alternative to expensive high-field scanners. Diffusion models, particularly the elucidated diffusion model (EDM) framework, have become popular for image generation and restoration due to their stable training and high sample quality. U-Net is the standard backbone for these models; 3D U-Nets process volumetric data directly, while 2.5D U-Nets process individual slices with neighboring slice conditioning, balancing performance and computational cost.

## [Restricted VMBQC model cuts parameter scaling for generative modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11578) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.11578v2) proposes a restricted variational measurement-based quantum computation (VMBQC) model that extends the unitary setting to a channel-based one using only a single additional trainable parameter, instead of doubling the parameter count to N×D. The authors show numerically and algebraically that this minimal extension suffices to generate probability distributions that the corresponding unitary model cannot learn. This work addresses a key limitation of VMBQC for generative modeling—the doubled parameter scaling that hampers trainability—by showing that a single extra parameter can unlock non-unitary generative power. It could make VMBQC-based generative models more practical and easier to optimize, benefiting researchers working on quantum generative models. The original VMBQC channel model scales as N×D parameters (N logical qubits, D depth), while the restricted model adds only one trainable parameter to the unitary model. The proof combines numerical experiments and algebraic arguments to demonstrate a separation in learnable distributions between the restricted channel model and the unitary model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Measurement-based quantum computation (MBQC) is a quantum computing paradigm where computation proceeds via single-qubit measurements on a highly entangled resource state (e.g., a cluster state), rather than via unitary gates. Because measurement outcomes are random, they are usually corrected through classical processing to ensure deterministic computation. Variational MBQC (VMBQC) instead exploits this measurement-induced randomness as a resource for generative modeling, but its channel model doubles the number of parameters compared to the unitary case, which can make optimization harder.

## [New method jointly learns temporal ordering and lags for causal discovery from time series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04081) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a decomposable BIC-style objective score for mixed data that combines node-specific sample sizes with a penalty favoring recent lags, and optimizes it using a Tabu search augmented with change-lag moves and greedy per-child lag tuning. The method jointly learns a temporal ordering and a dominant lag for each selected ordered variable pair, and on a UK COVID-19 dataset, 46.8% of selected edges had lags greater than one. This work addresses the challenging problem of learning causal Bayesian networks from time-series data where dependencies may arise at different time lags, providing a computationally tractable subroutine for edge-specific single-lag optimization. It could benefit researchers in causal discovery and time-series analysis, particularly those dealing with mixed data and variable lags. The method assumes no contemporaneous edges and causal sufficiency, and the paper establishes acyclicity and complexity results and derives recovery limits when effects are distributed across multiple lags. In simulations, it outperforms an inflexible lag-1 score-based learner in most settings, but specialized methods achieve higher recovery accuracy when their assumptions are satisfied.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Causal Bayesian Networks (CBNs) are graphical models that represent cause-and-effect relationships among variables and support reasoning under uncertainty. Learning the structure of a CBN from data is challenging, and time-series data adds complexity because dependencies can occur at different time lags. The BIC score is a common criterion for structure learning that balances model fit with complexity, and Tabu search is a metaheuristic optimization method often used for searching network structures.

A new arXiv paper presents a systematic evaluation framework for image-to-translation pipelines on medieval Latin manuscripts, showing that domain-specific OCR models reduce character error rate by up to 4.3× compared to general-purpose VLMs. It also introduces the Interpres-Parallel-Corpus (IPC), a dataset of 1,383 aligned manuscript image lines, transcriptions, and expert translations, claimed to be the first of its kind for medieval Latin. The findings highlight a specialization gap: smaller, domain-specific OCR models can outperform much larger general-purpose VLMs on historical documents, offering practical guidance for deploying translation systems in low-resource settings. This is relevant for digital humanities scholars and NLP researchers working with historical manuscripts. The paper uncovers a complexity paradox: the simplest pipeline—a specialized OCR model feeding directly into a VLM—outperforms all multi-component variants, while adding retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) or post-OCR correction introduces prompt saturation and error propagation that degrade translation quality. The evaluation uses the CATMuS Latin dataset and focuses on challenges like scribal shorthand, ligatures, and parchment degradation.

## [Input Pathways Affect Few-Shot, Not Zero-Shot, Binding in Tiny Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04926) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An exhaustive study of ~6-10K-parameter transformers on finite factored worlds finds that no informative input pathway (symbolic tokens, oracle code, or perceptual vector) achieves above-chance zero-shot compositional binding, while few-shot binding efficiency tracks code readability. The paper also withdraws a prior claim about pathway parameter sharing, attributing the effect to the initialization scale of the broadcast-added state. This work provides a rigorous, fully-enumerable testbed for isolating how input representation shapes compositional generalization in transformers, showing that zero-shot failure stems from inductive bias rather than missing information. It offers mechanistic insights that could inform architecture and training choices for systematic generalization, though the tiny model scale limits immediate practical impact. The study uses information-matched routes with an exact Bayes ceiling of 1.0, and shows endpoint invariance holds even when the state occupies its own sequence position. Failure anatomy differs: symbolic routes lose the answer at the readout, while index routes mis-bind, with the answer still decodable but output tracking the wrong slot.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Compositional binding is the ability to combine known components in novel ways, a key challenge for transformers. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural network computations, and this paper uses tiny transformers on exhaustively enumerated finite worlds to study how input format (symbolic tokens, clean oracle codes, or entangled perceptual vectors) affects binding. Inductive bias refers to the model's built-in assumptions that guide learning beyond the training data.

The framework uses a medical-literature-informed emotion taxonomy and updates emotional states through trigger identification, structured CPM appraisal, and emotion-state integration, and it is evaluated via student-led conversations, controlled trigger cases, nursing-expert assessments, LLM-judge comparisons, component ablations, and backbone checks. This arXiv paper proposes a personalized bilevel optimization framework that formalizes cloud-edge LLM collaboration as a dual structure, with the upper level optimizing edge-side personalization and the lower level governing cloud-side knowledge transfer. The authors introduce CIDERS, which embeds global trajectories into each local personalization step via consensus-variate correction, and report accuracy improvements of up to 200%+ on Qwen2.5-3B and Qwen2-1.5B. The work addresses a real tension in distributed LLM deployment: maintaining a unified knowledge foundation on the cloud while enabling domain-specific adaptation at the edge. If validated, such bilevel coordination could make cloud-edge LLM systems more practical for privacy-sensitive and latency-constrained applications. The paper provides a geometric characterization of the local trajectory and a full convergence guarantee, revealing an explicit trade-off between personalization and global convergence. Control-state diagnostics show strong preservation of the local orthogonal component, but a separate cross-domain evaluation finds only a small matched-domain response advantage, suggesting benefits may be domain-specific.

Bilevel optimization is a hierarchical optimization paradigm where an upper-level problem depends on the solution of a lower-level problem, and it is widely used to model meta-learning and hyperparameter tuning. This paper applies bilevel optimization to coordinate cloud-side knowledge transfer with edge-side personalization for LLMs.
