A new study evaluates StyleGAN2's intermediate latent space (w-space) as a parameterization method for ensemble-based data assimilation of non-Gaussian reservoir facies, comparing it against VAE-GAN and latent diffusion models. Results show that using w-space yields better history matching than the traditional z-space because w-space is more linear and disentangled, making it more compatible with the linear updates in ESMDA. Ensemble smoothers are widely used for history matching but degrade with non-Gaussian facies due to their Gaussian assumptions. This work identifies StyleGAN2's w-space as a particularly effective parameterization, offering a practical path to improve reservoir characterization and uncertainty quantification in complex geological settings. The study tested two 2D case studies—one categorical with three facies and one continuous—and validated results using standard geostatistical and history matching metrics. All three generative models were highly efficient, but StyleGAN2 stood out for geological realism and data matching, with w-space outperforming z-space due to its greater linearity and disentanglement.

StyleGAN2 is a generative adversarial network known for high-quality image synthesis, and its mapping network produces an intermediate w-space that is more disentangled than the initial z-space.

This paper introduces a time-uniform 1-bit mean-estimation primitive using randomized threshold queries and a clipped tail-integral identity, and embeds it into fixed-confidence best-arm identification algorithms. The lower bound reveals an intrinsic logarithmic penalty due to finite-variance 1-bit feedback, and the phased algorithm matches this up to logarithmic factors.

CARE-Drive introduces a coupled alternating refinement approach that decomposes ego-trajectory refinement into waypoint residual correction and position-conditioned heading generation for vision-language-action autonomous driving models. On NAVSIM v1, it achieves 88.8 PDMS, improving by 2.4 points over the unrefined base trajectories from the same backbone. This work shows that physically specialized refinement—separating position and heading—can yield measurable gains in trajectory quality for end-to-end autonomous driving, potentially influencing how future VLA models structure their output refinement. It matters for researchers and practitioners working on imitation learning and reinforcement fine-tuning for driving policies. The backbone is frozen while two experts are optimized alternately through supervised initialization and reinforcement fine-tuning; the position expert predicts bounded metric waypoint residuals, and the heading expert reconstructs orientation from adjacent angular increments conditioned on the corrected path. Ablation studies support the effectiveness of specialized experts and staged training.

## [JudgeMix: Judge-Guided Mixed-Rollout Data Synthesis for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16174) ⭐️ 6.0/10

JudgeMix introduces a judge-guided framework that dynamically interleaves target model and expert rollouts based on step-wise semantic evaluations, querying expert guidance only when the target model struggles. It also reweights expert supervision on different steps to preserve the model's correct behavior while correcting errors, achieving up to 10 pass@1 points improvement on AppWorld and AutomationBench. This work addresses the training-inference distribution mismatch in supervised fine-tuning for LLM agents, a key challenge that limits agent performance in real-world tasks. By synthesizing data that aligns with the target model's native state distribution, JudgeMix could enable more efficient and effective fine-tuning of LLM agents, impacting researchers and practitioners developing agentic systems. The method uses a judge to perform step-wise semantic evaluations, triggering expert intervention only when needed and reweighting expert supervision to avoid overwriting correct actions. Experiments on two agentic tool-use benchmarks show consistent improvements over existing methods, with gains up to 10 pass@1 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on expert trajectories is a common way to train LLM agents, but it suffers from distribution mismatch because the expert's state distribution differs from the target model's during inference. Recent approaches incorporate expert guidance through mixed rollouts or dense step-wise supervision, but existing methods apply expert assistance blindly, either disrupting rollouts with random interventions or overwriting correct actions with uniform supervision.

## [HyBridge: Bridge-Guided Hyperbolic Hypergraph Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16351) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HyBridge is a self-supervised hyperbolic hypergraph representation learning framework that estimates node bridge scores using the external connectivity of individual hyperedges, then guides node radial positions in hyperbolic space accordingly. Experiments on 10 real-world hypergraphs show it outperforms 22 competing methods, with up to 9.82% improvement in node classification. This work addresses why simply porting hypergraph representation learning to hyperbolic space does not consistently help, by linking node structural roles (bridge nodes) to embedding radii. It could improve modeling of tree-like groupwise structures in domains like social networks, bioinformatics, and recommendation. The method estimates bridge scores via hyperedge external connectivity, learns node and hyperedge embeddings through hyperbolic hypergraph encoding, and uses contrastive learning to preserve node discriminability. Code is available at an anonymous repository for reproducibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 16, 05:00

**Background**: Hypergraphs generalize graphs by allowing hyperedges to connect any number of nodes, making them natural for modeling groupwise or high-order relationships. Hyperbolic space, with its negative curvature, efficiently represents tree-like and hierarchical structures, but prior work found that naively extending hypergraph representation learning to hyperbolic space does not consistently improve performance. Bridge nodes are nodes that connect different communities or components, and their structural role is well studied in ordinary graphs but less so in hypergraphs.
